[^cite_note-Gallup-2]

Protests, including rallies, demonstrations, campaigns, and vigils, relating to the [Gaza war](74998519.md) have occurred nationwide across the United States since the conflict's start on October 7, 2023, occurring as part of a broader phenomenon of [Gaza war protests](75044900.md) around the world.

In the first ten days of the war, pro-Israeli demonstrations in support of Israel in the wake of the [Hamas-led attack](74998317.md) and focusing on the [Israeli hostage crisis](75025451.md) were most common,[^cite_note-DCdemand-3] but they have since been outnumbered by pro-Palestinian anti-war protests [calling for a ceasefire](75313349.md) and an end to the [Israeli occupation](2781576.md).[^cite_note-4][^cite_note-\:8-5] Pro-Palestinian protestors criticized [US military and diplomatic support to Israel](65284610.md), [Israel's invasion](74998519.md) of the [Gaza Strip](12047.md), Israel's [genocide of Gazans](75668427.md), and other Israeli [war crimes](75113830.md).[^cite_note-\:9-6] These protests included Jewish groups and some US administration staffers.

By December 5, more than 1 million Americans had participated in protest over the conflict, across over 2,600 events: 442 in support of Israel, and 2,100 in support of Palestine.[^cite_note-7][^cite_note-\:8-5] An escalation of [pro-Palestinian protests on university campuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_at_universities#Protests_on_campuses) began on April 17, 2024, and [continued into 2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_at_universities#In_2025).

Four people have [self-immolated](916669.md) in protest of the war: an unidentified woman draped in a Palestinian flag on December 1, 2023, in [Atlanta](3138.md), Georgia, who survived with serious injures;[^cite_note-8] and [Aaron Bushnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-immolation_of_Aaron_Bushnell), a 25-year-old serviceman of the [United States Air Force](32090.md), in [Washington, D.C.](108956.md), died in hospital on February 25, 2024.[^cite_note-aaronbushnell-9] The third by anti-war activist Matt Nelson occurred in [Boston](24437894.md) in September 2024.[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-11] The fourth occurred in Washington, D.C., in October 2024.[^cite_note-12]

## National

Pro-Palestine protests were held throughout the United States on [Black Friday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Friday_\(shopping\)) with activists calling to "shut it down" for Palestine.[^cite_note-13] There were protests in Los Angeles;[^cite_note-14] Chicago;[^cite_note-15] [Troy, Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troy,_Michigan);[^cite_note-16] [Saint Louis, Missouri](27687.md);[^cite_note-17] [Estero, Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estero,_Florida);[^cite_note-18] and many other cities.

As of December 5, according to Harvard's Nonviolent Action Lab and the Crowd Counting Consortium, more than 1 million Americans had participated in protests over the conflict.[^cite_note-19]

"Ceasefire carols" were organized by activists throughout the United States leading up to Christmas 2023. Sarah Abbott who helped organize a [carol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_carol) outside Senator [Amy Klobuchar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amy_Klobuchar)'s home was quoted in [CBS Minnesota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WCCO-TV) saying, "Our government is funding this genocide, white Christians have historically and currently played major roles in the perpetuation of [Zionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionism), and as people of conscience, we can't stand by silently."[^cite_note-\:3-20] Ceasefire carols were sung in [Minneapolis, Minnesota](6097240.md); [Louisville, Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisville,_Kentucky);[^cite_note-21] [Salt Lake City, Utah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salt_Lake_City);[^cite_note-22] and in at least 17 other cities.[^cite_note-\:3-20]

On December 27, protesters blocked airports, including at [John F. Kennedy International Airport](97218.md) and [Los Angeles International Airport](18131.md).[^cite_note-23][^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25]

In February 2024, a joint report by the [Center for Constitutional Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Constitutional_Rights) and [Palestine Legal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Legal) found widespread legal attacks against pro-Palestinian activism, describing it as "a dangerous attack on constitutionally protected speech and association".[^cite_note-26]

In response to actions taken by the [Biden administration](65732174.md) in support of Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md)'s approach to the war, [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_the_United_States) and [Arab Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Americans) organized a campaign titled "Abandon Biden" to advocate against voting for Biden in the [2024 presidential election](59568546.md).[^cite_note-27] Leaders from the [United Electrical Workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Electrical_Workers) and [United Auto Workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Auto_Workers) vowed to use their electoral clout to push for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-28]

On March 31, 2024, USAF airman [Larry Hebert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larry_Hebert), a member of [Veterans for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veterans_for_Peace), began a hunger strike to bring attention to starving children in Gaza.[^cite_note-29]

The [Tax Day protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_pro-Palestinian_Tax_Day_protests) were held on Tax Day (April 15). After the police crackdown of the [2024 Columbia University pro-Palestinian campus occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_at_Columbia_University) on April 18,[^cite_note-30] Gaza solidarity encampments spread to multiple campuses across the US, including MIT, Washington University in St. Louis, University of Maryland, and Tufts University.[^cite_note-31]

On February 9, 2025, a protester carrying a Palestinian flag interrupted [Kendrick Lamar](29909823.md)'s performance during the [Super Bowl LIX halftime show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LIX_halftime_show).

### 2024 presidential election events

See also: [Gaza war protest vote movements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protest_vote_movements) and [Joe Biden 2024 presidential campaign § Opposition among Arab and Muslim Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden_2024_presidential_campaign#Opposition_among_Arab_and_Muslim_Americans)

Following the [2023 Hamas-led attack on Israel](74998317.md) and the [Gaza humanitarian crisis](75277791.md), President Biden has faced sustained opposition and event disruptions from a vocal minority[^cite_note-Lerer_March282024-32] of progressives, Arab Americans, and Muslims over his handling of the conflict and the creation of a [protest vote movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protest_vote_movements) against him.[^cite_note-Lerer_March282024-32]

At a campaign rally in Virginia on January 23, 2024, incumbent president Joe Biden was interrupted more than a dozen times by protesters calling for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-33] The following day, he was interrupted by anti-war protesters in Washington, D.C., during an endorsement rally held by the [United Auto Workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Auto_Workers).[^cite_note-34] Following these protests, the Biden campaign took "extraordinary" steps to prevent pro-Palestinian protesters from interrupting the president, including avoiding college campuses, hiring private companies to vet attendees, and not disclosing his events' locations ahead of time.[^cite_note-35] On March 10, a protester at a Biden campaign event interrupted his speech, stating, "You're a dictator, genocide Joe. Tens of thousands of Palestinians are dead. Children are dying."[^cite_note-36] Protesters interrupted Biden speaking on healthcare in North Carolina, telling him, "Hospitals in Gaza are being bombed," to which Biden replied, "They have a point. We need to get a lot more care into Gaza."[^cite_note-37]

On March 28, Biden held a fundraiser for his presidential campaign at [Radio City Music Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_City_Music_Hall), accompanied by former Presidents Barack Obama and Bill Clinton, as well as other celebrities. Biden and the former presidents were repeatedly heckled during the event by pro-Palestine protesters, and a demonstration was held outside the venue to protest the Biden administration's policies in Gaza. According to [Within Our Lifetime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Within_Our_Lifetime) organizer [Nerdeen Kiswani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nerdeen_Kiswani), about 1,000 were in attendance.[^cite_note-38] In April 2024, Biden cancelled a planned [Iftar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iftar) due to pushback from invited Muslim American attendees.[^cite_note-39] At the 2024 [White House Correspondents' Dinner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House_Correspondents'_Dinner), protesters shouted "Shame on you!" at arriving attendees.[^cite_note-40]

### Pro-Palestinian protests on university campuses

Further information: [Gaza war protests at universities § Protests on campuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_at_universities#Protests_on_campuses)

[Map](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:Map/3/38.828175/-96.5795/en)

Universities in the United States with [Gaza War](74998519.md) protests since April 2024. Columbia University is marked in red. Other colleges that had encampments are marked in green, and non-encampment protests are marked in blue.

These paragraphs are an excerpt from [Gaza war protests at universities § Protests on campuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_at_universities#Protests_on_campuses).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Gaza_war_protests_at_universities\&action=edit)]

By May 6, 2024, [student protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Student_protest) had occurred in 45 of the 50 states [in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses_in_the_United_States_in_2024) and the District of Columbia, with encampments, [occupations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupation_\(protest\)), [walkouts](2016860.md) or [sit-ins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sit-in) on almost 140 campuses.[^cite_note-41] Thirty-four encampments were established in the United Kingdom;[^cite_note-42] across universities in Australia,[^cite_note-43] beginning with the [University of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Sydney);[^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_universities_Dumas-2024-44] and in Canada, including an [encampment at McGill University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McGill_University_pro-Palestinian_encampment).[^cite_note-45][^cite_note-46] On May 7, protests spread further on European campuses after mass arrests at the [University of Amsterdam campus occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_University_of_Amsterdam_pro-Palestinian_campus_occupations),[^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_universities_AP_News-2024a-47] including occupation of campus buildings at [Leipzig University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leipzig_University) in Germany, [Sciences Po](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Sciences_Po_pro-Palestinian_occupation_protest) in France, and [Ghent University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghent_University) in Belgium.[^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_universities_Kassam-2024-48] By May 8, protests had taken place in more than 25 countries,[^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_universities_Al_Jazeera-2024c2-49] and on May 13, approximately 1,000 Dutch students and university staff took part in a national [walk-out](2016860.md).[^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_universities_Sky_News-50]

### Queer pro-Palestinian Protests

See also: [LGBTQ advocacy in the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_advocacy_in_the_Gaza_war)

During [Pride parades](58168.md) throughout 2024, pro-Palestinian protesters counter-protested against celebration. Many often disrupted parades, leading to significant controversy.[^cite_note-51]

## Midwest



Pro-Palestine protest in Columbus, Ohio, December 22, 2023

### Illinois

Further information: [Gaza war protests at the 2024 Democratic National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_at_the_2024_Democratic_National_Convention)

An October 14 protest in Chicago called for the freeing of [hostages taken by Hamas](75025451.md), peace for Israelis and Palestinians, and an end to all killings of civilians.[^cite_note-wls-52][^cite_note-53] On October 24, two men were arrested after one shot into the air and another [pepper-sprayed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pepper-sprayed) pro-Palestinian demonstrators at an Israel solidarity event near Chicago.[^cite_note-54] On [Black Friday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Friday_\(shopping\)), about 1,000 protesters marched on the [Magnificent Mile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnificent_Mile) retail district to condemn violence in Gaza. Police shut down streets and snowplows were used to corral the protesters.[^cite_note-55] On the day before Christmas Eve, a large car caravan organized by a pro-Palestinian group blocked the [I-90](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_90) near [O'Hare International Airport](248449.md).[^cite_note-56] On New Year's Eve, hundreds of pro-Palestinian marched in Chicago, blocking the I-90 again, as well as the [I-94](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_94).[^cite_note-57] On January 5, 2024, protesters at [Chicago Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Union_Station) demonstrated for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-58]

On August 19, thousands protested near the [United Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Center) on the first day of the [2024 Democratic National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Democratic_National_Convention). Some tore down security fences while more chanted "End the occupation now!" and "[The whole world is watching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_whole_world_is_watching)!" as a nod to anti-Vietnam War protesters at the [1968 Democratic National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968_Democratic_National_Convention) in 1968.[^cite_note-59]

### Indiana

Pro-Palestinian protesters march through a street in downtown Bloomington on May 7, 2024.

Pro-Palestinian protesters in [Bloomington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomington,_Indiana) on May 7, 2024

Pro-Israel and pro-Palestinian protests and demonstrations occurred on each side of the [Soldiers' and Sailors' Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soldiers'_and_Sailors'_Monument_\(Indianapolis\)) in [Indianapolis](57707.md) on October 12, 2023.[^cite_note-60] A pro-Palestinian protest took place on January 8, 2024, also at the Soldiers' and Sailors' Monument, where nearly 30 people attended before increasing to about 60 people, after moving to protest outside United States senator [Todd Young](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Todd_Young)'s residence.[^cite_note-61]

On April 25, a pro-Palestine protest occurred outside the residence of state governor [Eric Holcomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Holcomb), which resulted in the arrests of fourteen people.[^cite_note-\:7-62] Additionally, protesters obstructed [Meridian Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meridian_Street_\(Indianapolis\)) in Indianapolis on the same day, which led to the [Indianapolis Metropolitan Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Metropolitan_Police_Department) threatening arrests on them.[^cite_note-\:7-62]

In September 2024, two people were charged with blocking the driveway of a [Raytheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raytheon) facility in [Fort Wayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Wayne,_Indiana), giving letters to employees urging them to find a different use for their products. Charges were dropped in December.[^cite_note-63]

### Iowa

A pro-Palestine rally was held in Cowles Commons in downtown [Des Moines](9163.md) on October 10 with approximately 80 people attending.[^cite_note-64]

On October 25, hundreds of [Grinnell College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grinnell_College) students walked out demanding that the college stop any financial support of Israel.[^cite_note-65] On November 4, more than 100 [University of Iowa](241119.md) students and community members from across Iowa gathered to rally for Palestine, calling for justice, peace and freedom from the oppression of Israel.[^cite_note-66] On December 17, nearly 150 protesters gathered in two separate demonstrations in front of [Terrace Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrace_Hill), the official residence of the governor of Iowa, [Kim Reynolds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Reynolds), as she was hosting a Christmas party. One demonstration was pro-Palestine, titled "No Justice No Christmas", while the other was in support of Iowa's Area Education Agencies (AEAs), with 40 people attending. Other protesters spoke out against recent [anti-LGBTQ+ legislation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Iowa) in the state and a local drag artist performed.[^cite_note-67]

### Kansas

On November 14, 500 flags memorializing 10,000 Palestinians who died from October 7 to November 14 were placed on the lawn of Watson Library at the [University of Kansas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Kansas). The event was organized by the Muslim Student Association and [Al-Hadaf KC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Hadaf_KC?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-68] The Muslim Student Association and Middle Eastern Student Association organized a student walk-out for Palestine on November 20 with the goal of the chancellor of the university, [Douglas Girod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doug_Girod), releasing a statement in solidarity with Palestinian people.[^cite_note-69] On November 30, pro-Palestine activists organized a sit-in at Strong Hall on the University of Kansas campus and hung banners inside and outside of the building. Protesters called again for a statement from the chancellor and criticized the university's involvement in the [Defense Advanced Research Projects Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DARPA) and [U.S.-Israel Binational Science Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_%E2%80%93_Israel_Binational_Science_Foundation).[^cite_note-70][^cite_note-71] Over 100 activists marched down [Massachusetts Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Street) on March 2, 2024, calling for "hands off [Rafah](584373.md)", for more humanitarian aid, and to vote [uncommitted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uncommitted_\(voting_option\)) in the [2024 Democratic Party presidential primary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Democratic_Party_presidential_primaries).[^cite_note-72]

On the first day of [Chanukah](7388.md), December 7, around two dozen activists from Jewish Voice for Peace sat in [Sharice Davids'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharice_Davids) [Overland Park, Kansas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overland_Park,_Kansas), office calling for a ceasefire and for more humanitarian aid to Gaza. Protesters carried signs saying, "Chanukah for ceasefire" and "Stop funding genocide".[^cite_note-73][^cite_note-74]

### Michigan

A group of pro-ceasefire activists gathered at the [Gerald R. Ford Federal Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerald_R._Ford_Federal_Building?action=edit\&redlink=1) in [Grand Rapids, Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Rapids,_Michigan), on November 7, resulting in three arrests for people who refused to leave the office of Representative [Hillary Scholten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Scholten).[^cite_note-75] In Detroit, a Thanksgiving Day parade was interrupted by about 200 pro-Palestine protesters.[^cite_note-76] On February 1, 2024, a group of [Arab Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Americans) protested against the arrival of Joe Biden for a campaign stop in [Dearborn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dearborn,_Michigan).[^cite_note-77]

On the 2024 [Quds Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quds_Day) (April 5), a pro-Palestine rally was organised in Dearborn (a city considered the capital of [Arab Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Americans)), where some protesters chanted "[Death to America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_to_America)" and "[Death to Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_to_Israel)".[^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79][^cite_note-80] The Al Quds Committee Detroit, who organised the rally, posted on Facebook that the chants were "wrongful" and "a mistake", but that they will also continue to criticise the [foreign policy of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_foreign_policy_in_the_Middle_East).[^cite_note-81]

Mayor [Abdullah Hammoud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdullah_Hammoud) spoke at a Dearborn rally on September 24, 2024, immediately following activist [Osama Siblani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_Siblani). The two embraced as Hammoud joined Siblani on the stage. Siblani called to "return \[Israelis] to Poland" and praised [Hezbollah](13919.md) leader [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md). Hammoud did not address the praise for the Hezbollah leader or the crowd's calls of "death to Israel", and did not respond to the media's request for comment.[^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83]

### Minnesota



Pro-Palestinian protesters in [Minneapolis](6097240.md) on November 4, 2023

On October 10, a pro-Israel solidarity gathering took place at the Beth el Synagogue in [St. Louis Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Louis_Park,_Minnesota). U.S. Senator [Amy Klobuchar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amy_Klobuchar) spoke: "They are trying to destroy Israel. We will not let that happen."[^cite_note-84] On October 18, a large crowd gathered outside the [Minnesota State Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnesota_State_Capitol) to support Palestinians in St. Paul.[^cite_note-85] On October 22, in Minneapolis, a pro-Palestinian rally took place on the [Irene Hixon Whitney Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minneapolis_Sculpture_Garden). A car drove through the crowd in the afternoon and a confrontation occurred.[^cite_note-86] A man was arrested in [Minneapolis](6097240.md) after driving through a crowd of anti-war activists.[^cite_note-87] On November 1, a protester interrupted a speech by [Joe Biden](145422.md) in Minneapolis asking him to call for a ceasefire, leading him to respond that a humanitarian "pause" was needed.[^cite_note-88] On November 11, hundreds of activists marched Cathedral Hill to the state capitol demanding a cease-fire.[^cite_note-89]

Seventy pro-ceasefire protesters demonstrated outside of Democratic presidential candidate and congressman [Dean Phillips'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dean_Phillips) office in [Minnetonka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnetonka,_Minnesota) on December 6.[^cite_note-90] Hundreds protested for a ceasefire outside Amy Klobuchar's office on January 8.[^cite_note-91]

On June 1, [Twin Ports DSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Socialists_of_America) held a rally outside [Duluth City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duluth_Civic_Center_Historic_District). Speakers represented Palestinian, Indigenous, Muslim and Jewish viewpoints. They demanded a ceasefire in Gaza, repeal of the Minnesota Anti-Boycott, and Sanction Law and end of all aid to Israel.[^cite_note-92] The group then marched to the [Duluth Entertainment Convention Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duluth_Entertainment_Convention_Center) where the [Minnesota Democratic-Farmer-Labor Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnesota_Democratic%E2%80%93Farmer%E2%80%93Labor_Party) (DFL) was endorsing candidates and selecting delegates for the upcoming [Democratic National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Democratic_National_Convention).[^cite_note-93][^cite_note-94][^cite_note-95]

### Missouri



Pro-Palestine activists rally by the road at Mill Creek Park in [Kansas City, Missouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas_City,_Missouri), October 14, 2023

On November 6, more than 75 activists blocked the entrances to the [Boeing](18933266.md) plant in the [St. Louis](27687.md) suburb of [St. Charles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Charles,_Missouri), where the company manufactures bombs that have been sold to the Israeli government.[^cite_note-96][^cite_note-97] About 150 activists participated in a second protest, blocking entrances again on November 21.[^cite_note-98][^cite_note-99] Pro-Palestine students at [Washington University in St. Louis (Washu)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_University_in_St._Louis) staged a [die-in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Die-in) to pressure the university to cut ties with Boeing.[^cite_note-100] Student activists from [University of Missouri-St. Louis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Missouri%E2%80%93St._Louis), Washu, and [St. Louis University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Louis_University) called for their universities to divest and disaffiliate with Boeing over the company's weapons manufacturing in a letter endorsed by seven student groups.[^cite_note-101]

About 600 Kansas City residents attended a pro-Israel rally on October 9.[^cite_note-102] On October 15, hundreds of people gathered for a pro-Palestine protest at Mill Creek Park.[^cite_note-103][^cite_note-104] On November 8, 30 activists associated with [Al-Hadaf KC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Hadaf_KC?action=edit\&redlink=1) and [KC Tenants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas_City_Tenants_Union) held a sit-in outside [Representative Emanuel Cleaver II's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emanuel_Cleaver) office calling for his support for the [Ceasefire Now Resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceasefire_Now_Resolution).[^cite_note-105] On November 18, Cleaver called for a ceasefire, but did not sign onto the resolution.[^cite_note-106] In February 2024, the [Girl Scouts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Girl_Scouts_of_the_USA) threatened to sue a Missouri chapter for making bracelets for starving children in Gaza, leading the chapter to leave the national organization.[^cite_note-107]

### Ohio



Officers arresting a pro-Palestinian protester at the OSU Gaza Solidarity Encampment, April 25, 2024

These paragraphs are an excerpt from [Gaza war protests at Ohio State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University\&action=edit)]

A series of protests at [Ohio State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_State_University) by [pro-Palestinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-Palestinian) demonstrators are ongoing and began on-campus following the outbreak of the [Gaza war](74998519.md) and [genocide](75668427.md). A solidarity encampment was constructed on OSU's South Oval on April 25, 2024 in solidarity with the [Columbia University encampment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Solidarity_Encampment_\(Columbia_University\)). [Riot police](1148542.md) then made 36 arrests against student protesters — the largest [mass arrests](28079790.md) in campus history since the [1969–1970 Vietnam War protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_State_University#1969%E2%80%931970_Vietnam_War_protests).[^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University_lanternApril25-108]

The protester demands of OSU include "[financial divestment, academic boycott, financial disclosure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boycott,_Divestment,_and_Sanctions), acknowledging the genocide, and ending targeted policing".[^cite_note-109][^cite_note-110][^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University_WOR-111]

Pro-Palestinian groups have been critical of the university's responses to the protests, which have included allowing [state troopers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_State_Highway_Patrol) to aim [sniper rifles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sniper_rifles) at students during the dispersal of the Gaza Solidarity Encampment,[^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University_snipers0-112][^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University_snipers1-113][^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University_snipers2-114][^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University_36Arrests-115] suspending a pro-Palestinian student organization,[^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University_\:0-116][^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University_campusRef-117][^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University_suspension-118] and blocking the Undergraduate Student Government's attempts at passing legislation for financial divestment from Israel after receiving pressure from officials in [Zionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionist) organization [Hillel International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillel_International).[^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University_\:1-119][^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University_leak-120][^cite_note-Gaza_war_protests_at_Ohio_State_University_divestblock-121]

Following protests, the OSU administration coordinated multi-agency police operations, implemented a campus-wide chalking ban, and [expelled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expulsion_\(education\)) pro-Palestinian social media influencer [Guy Christensen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guy_Christensen); state-level officials floated invoking a 1953 anti-[KKK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KKK) statute against pro-Palestinian protesters who wore face coverings, suggested terminating faculty members who encourage or engage in "violence on campus", and passed the "CAMPUS" Act to formalize [free speech restrictions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_speech_in_schools_in_the_United_States); and officials in the [Trump administration](78298108.md) revoked the student visa of at least one pro-Palestinian protester at OSU and threatened to withdraw funding from OSU if they did not sufficiently "combat antisemitism" on campus. These actions have been met with multiple lawsuits over alleged violations of [First](31653.md) and [Fourteenth Amendment](31666.md) rights.

The university has insisted their actions are politically neutral, with OSU President [Ted Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ted_Carter) stating the "university's long-standing space rules are content neutral and are enforced uniformly".[^cite_note-122] Critics argue the university engages in [selective enforcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selective_enforcement) of university space rules, enforcing policies disproportionately against pro-Palestinian groups and not similarly against other groups on campus.

## Northeast



Students protesting against the war in [Cambridge, Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambridge,_Massachusetts), October 19, 2023

In Merrimack, New Hampshire, three protesters were arrested and charged with riot, sabotage, criminal mischief, criminal trespass, and disorderly conduct for a pro-Palestine protest that occurred at the offices of Elbit Systems, an Israeli "defense and homeland security business".[^cite_note-123][^cite_note-124]

On November 11, pro-Palestinian protesters gathered near Biden's residence in [Wilmington, Delaware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilmington,_Delaware), calling for a ceasefire and accusing him of genocide.[^cite_note-125] On January 23, 2024, protesters outside a campaign event for Biden in [Manassas, Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manassas,_Virginia), chanted "Hey Hey, Ho Ho, Genocide Joe".[^cite_note-126] The [Council on American-Islamic Relations](159138.md) called for a federal probe into violations of international law due to a real estate event planned at a synagogue in [Teaneck, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teaneck,_New_Jersey), which would sell "stolen land" taken from Palestinians in the [Israeli-occupied](2781576.md) [West Bank](33209.md).[^cite_note-127]

On December 3, pro-Palestine demonstrators protested outside of Goldie, a [falafel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falafel) restaurant in [Philadelphia, Pennsylvania](50585.md). The Jewish shop owner was donating profits from the restaurant to Israel's Friends of [United Hatzalah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Hatzalah), an emergency medical fund established in the wake of the October 7 attacks.[^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129] Dozens of demonstrators were arrested protesting against the [Pennsylvania Treasury Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treasurer_of_Pennsylvania)'s investment in Israel bonds at the [Pennsylvania State Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_State_Capitol).[^cite_note-130]

### Connecticut

A protest was held at the [University of Connecticut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Connecticut)'s main campus in [Storrs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Storrs,_Connecticut) by the UConn SJP chapter.[^cite_note-131] On April 19, 2024, a pro-Palestinian protest occurred on the campus of [Yale University](34273.md) in [New Haven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Haven,_Connecticut).[^cite_note-132] Several days later, police arrested 45 protesters on the Yale campus.[^cite_note-133]

A pro-Palestinian protest was held outside the [Connecticut State Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connecticut_State_Capitol) in [Hartford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hartford,_Connecticut) on April 10, 2024.[^cite_note-134] In [Middletown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middletown,_Connecticut), ten protesters were arrested for blocking the entrance to the [Pratt & Whitney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pratt_&_Whitney) facility on April 15.[^cite_note-135]

### District of Columbia

#### Rallies

Further information: [National March on Washington: Free Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_March_on_Washington:_Free_Palestine), [March for Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_for_Israel), [2023 Democratic National Committee protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Democratic_National_Committee_protests), and [2024 visit by Benjamin Netanyahu to the United States § Public protests in Washington D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_visit_by_Benjamin_Netanyahu_to_the_United_States#Public_protests_in_Washington_D.C.)



Protest against the war in Gaza at the [White House](33057.md), Washington, D.C., October 8, 2023

Jewish activists protested for a [ceasefire](592591.md) on October 16 at the [White House](33057.md), saying [Kaddish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaddish), singing Jewish songs, and chanting "Not in our name". 49 were arrested: 16 for blocking entrances and 33 for crossing safety barriers.[^cite_note-136][^cite_note-137][^cite_note-\:9-6] On November 15, the headquarters of the [Democratic National Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_National_Committee) was evacuated by the [U.S. Capitol Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Capitol_Police) during a pro-Palestine rally, organized, in part, by [IfNotNow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IfNotNow), who reported that "police are being extremely violent" at the rally. Six officers were injured.[^cite_note-138] On November 27, a group of more than a dozen state lawmakers and activists, including actress and former-gubernatorial candidate [Cynthia Nixon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cynthia_Nixon), launched a [hunger strike](222292.md) at the White House calling for a permanent ceasefire.[^cite_note-139] On December 7, a group of doctors called for a ceasefire on [Capitol Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitol_Hill).[^cite_note-140] On December 11, a group of twenty Jewish senior citizens were arrested after chaining themselves to the White House fence in protest of the war.[^cite_note-141]

On November 4, 100,000 to 300,000 people participated in the "[National March on Washington: Free Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_March_on_Washington:_Free_Palestine)", marking the largest pro-Palestine protest in US history.[^cite_note-National_March_on_Washington-142] The march called for a ceasefire in Gaza, with many protesters condemning Israel's ongoing genocide in the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-\:02-143] It was organized by [A.N.S.W.E.R.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A.N.S.W.E.R.), [People's Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Forum), [Students for Justice in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Students_for_Justice_in_Palestine), and other organizations.[^cite_note-\:02-143][^cite_note-\:12-144] It saw support from over 450 organizations, including [Democratic Socialists of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Socialists_of_America), [Jewish Voice for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Voice_for_Peace), [IfNotNow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IfNotNow), and the [US Campaign for Palestinian Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Campaign_for_Palestinian_Rights).[^cite_note-145]



[March on Washington for Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_on_Washington_for_Gaza), January 2024

On November 14, 100,000 to 290,000 people participated in the "[March for Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_for_Israel)" which called for the release of Israeli hostages and denouncing antisemitism. Israeli President [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) spoke to the crowd through video feed, and the march lasted about two hours, according to organizers; the [Jewish Federations of North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Federations_of_North_America) and the [Conference of Presidents of Major American Jewish Organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conference_of_Presidents_of_Major_American_Jewish_Organizations).[^cite_note-March_for_Israel-146]

On January 13, 2024, thousands of Pro-Palestinian demonstrators gathered in Washington, D.C., to protest as part of a worldwide day of protests against the war.[^cite_note-147]

On June 8, 2024, protesters held a "red-line" banner as thousands of people participated in a rally for ceasefire at the White House.[^cite_note-148] The Biden administration slammed the "repugnant" rhetoric voiced at the rally, during which pro-Hamas and pro-[Hezbollah](13919.md) demonstrators called for jihad and the killing of Zionists.[^cite_note-149]

On October 5, 2024, more than a thousand protesters gathered in [Black Lives Matter Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Lives_Matter_Plaza) prior to the one year anniversary of October 7. During the protest, an attempted [self-immolation](916669.md) took place when a journalist shouting about [misinformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misinformation_in_the_Gaza_war) set their[^cite_note-150] arm on fire.[^cite_note-151][^cite_note-152]

#### Staffers

On November 8, more than 100 US congressional staffers staged a [walk-out](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walk-out), demanding a ceasefire.[^cite_note-153] A group of 40 [White House](33057.md) interns sent a letter to President Biden saying they would "no longer remain silent" about the [genocide](12441.md) occurring in Gaza.[^cite_note-154] Josh Paul, a senior State Department official, resigned in protest of the US policy toward the war.[^cite_note-155] On December 13, more than 100 staffers from the [Department of Homeland Security](58236.md) stated the department leadership had "turned a blind eye to the bombing of refugee camps, hospitals, ambulances, and civilians".[^cite_note-156] The same day, more than three dozen staffers in the Biden Administration held a rally outside the White House calling for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-157] On January 14, ahead of reports that federal employees were planning a walkout in opposition to U.S. government policy in Israel, Speaker of the House [Mike Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Johnson) stated the [House Oversight Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_Oversight_Committee) would "ensure that each federal agency initiates appropriate disciplinary proceedings against any person who walks out on their job".[^cite_note-158]

On May 15, [Lily Greenberg Call](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lily_Greenberg_Call), a special assistant to the Chief of Staff with the [Interior Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_the_Interior) became the first Jewish and fifth overall staffer to resign in protest of US support for Israel. Call alleged that Biden was using Jewish people to justify US involvement in the conflict.[^cite_note-159]

#### Officials' homes

Protesters demonstrated outside [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken)'s home accusing him of participating in genocide.[^cite_note-160] On December 12, hecklers interrupted a speech by vice-president [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris), demanding she call for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-161] A protest was held at [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin)'s home on December 25.[^cite_note-162] Protesters again demonstrated outside Blinken's home, encouraging passing cars to honk their horns.[^cite_note-163] In late-January 2024, protesters began camping outside of Blinken's house in protest of the war.[^cite_note-164]

#### Government buildings

On October 18, about 300 protesters were arrested at a Jewish-led pro-ceasefire demonstration inside the [Cannon House Office Building](840257.md).[^cite_note-DCdemand-3] On October 31, antiwar protesters interrupted a [US Senate](24909346.md) hearing where Secretary of State Antony Blinken and Defense Secretary Lloyd Austin requested billions of military aid for Israel.[^cite_note-165] On November 3, a pro-Palestine group reported that 52 activists had been arrested for engaging in [sit-ins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sit-in) in US Senate offices.[^cite_note-166] On December 11, 51 protesters calling for a ceasefire in the Senate building were arrested.[^cite_note-167] On December 19, protesters entered the [US Capitol Building](31979.md) to protest against the war.[^cite_note-168] On January 17, 150 members from Mennonites Action were arrested at a pro-ceasefire sit-in in the US Capitol.[^cite_note-169] On January 14, 2024, protesters organized by [Code Pink](2440600.md) threw baby dolls covered in fake blood at the White House as part of the "National March 4 Gaza".[^cite_note-170] In March 2024, protesters blocked [Pennsylvania Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Avenue) in protest of Biden's [2024 State of the Union Address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_State_of_the_Union_Address), with one demonstrator stating, "No more genocide with our tax dollars".[^cite_note-171] On July 23, a day before [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) was scheduled to address a [joint session of Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_session_of_Congress), the US Capitol Police announced that they had arrested about 200 protesters wearing red shirts that said "JEWS SAY STOP ARMING ISRAEL" in the Cannon House Office Building. Jewish Voice for Peace, who organized the sit-in, said that at least 400 were arrested.[^cite_note-172] On March 4, 2026, former Marine [Brian McGinnis](82544763.md) interrupted a hearing in the [Hart Senate Office Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hart_Senate_Office_Building) with shouts of “America does not want to send its sons and daughters to war for Israel!” US senator [Tim Sheehy](77920271.md) (R-MT) attempted to assist [Capitol Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitol_police) in forcibly removing him, breaking his arm in the process.[^cite_note-173]

#### Aaron Bushnell

Main article: [Self-immolation of Aaron Bushnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-immolation_of_Aaron_Bushnell)



A vigil to [Aaron Bushnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-immolation_of_Aaron_Bushnell) held outside of the Israeli embassy in Washington, D.C., February 2024

On February 25, 2024, Aaron Bushnell,[^cite_note-aaronbushnell-9] an active-duty U.S. Air Force officer, self-immolated outside of the [Israeli Embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_Israel,_Washington,_D.C.) in Washington, D.C., protesting Israel's assault on Gaza during the war. Bushnell filmed the protest and livestreamed it on [Twitch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitch_\(service\)), and recorded as he walked up to the Israeli Embassy, saying "I am an active duty member of the United States Air Force, and I will no longer be complicit to [genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_genocide_accusation). I am about to engage in an extreme act of protest. But compared to what people have been experiencing in Palestine at their hands of their colonizers, it's not extreme at all. This is what our ruling class has decided will be normal."[^cite_note-174] When Bushnell arrived at the embassy gates, he set his phone down to film himself dousing his body in a clear liquid from a metal bottle. He then lit himself on fire while shouting "Free [Palestine](241405.md)".[^cite_note-gazagenocide-175][^cite_note-176] Multiple police officers responded to the scene and used [fire extinguishers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_extinguisher) on Bushnell. He was transported by the [DC Fire and EMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_of_Columbia_Fire_and_Emergency_Medical_Services_Department) to a local hospital where he later died from his injuries.[^cite_note-wapo-177] A vigil was held on February 27 in front of the Israeli Embassy to memorialize Bushnell.[^cite_note-178]

### Maine

On [Black Friday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Friday_\(shopping\)) 2023, the Maine Coalition for Palestine held a rally near a shopping mall in [Freeport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freeport,_Maine).[^cite_note-179]

On July 27, 2024, at least nine protesters were arrested during a christening event for the new [Arleigh Burke-class destroyer](197341.md), [USS Patrick Gallagher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Patrick_Gallagher), for blocking the road outside the [Bath Iron Works](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bath_Iron_Works). Two were charged with obstructing a public way and the others were charged with criminal trespass.[^cite_note-180] Police from Bath and Sagadahoc County arrested the group which had split off from a larger demonstration of about 75 people.[^cite_note-181]

### Massachusetts



Pro-Israel rally in [Boston](24437894.md), Massachusetts, October 9, 2023

The [Students for Justice in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Students_for_Justice_in_Palestine) organized a protest at [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University) in October, several days after numerous students were [doxed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doxed) for publicly supporting Palestine.[^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183] A pro-Palestinian [die-in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Die-in) took place at [Harvard College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_College) on October 18. A [proctor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proctor) in charge of overseeing freshmen was indefinitely relieved of his duties for participating.[^cite_note-184] On November 9, Harvard Rabbi [Hirschy Zarchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hirschy_Zarchi) spoke at a demonstration to pay tribute to the hostages held by Hamas, saying that anyone who justified the October 7 attack was "not just an animal. You're below an animal. You're a monster".[^cite_note-185] During a pro-ceasefire sit-in protest at the [University of Massachusetts Amherst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Massachusetts_Amherst), 56 students and one university employee were arrested for trespassing.[^cite_note-186] Students staged another die-in at the UMass Amherst on December 6.[^cite_note-187] Anti-war student protesters at [MIT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MIT) were suspended for refusing to leave a campus building they were occupying.[^cite_note-188]

On Black Friday, protesters in [Somerville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somerville,_Massachusetts) targeted a [PUMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puma_\(brand\)) store because the company provides equipment to the Israeli National Sports Teams.[^cite_note-189] On [Thanksgiving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanksgiving_\(United_States\)), President Biden was heckled by pro-Palestine activists in [Nantucket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nantucket) chanting "Free Palestine".[^cite_note-190][^cite_note-191] On October 24, hundreds of pro-Palestinian protesters gathered in front of [Worcester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worcester,_Massachusetts) City Hall.[^cite_note-192] On the same day, in Nantucket, protesters targeted a Christmas tree lighting attended by Biden and [his wife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jill_Biden).[^cite_note-193] Hundreds of pro-Palestine protesters rallied outside of a fundraiser for the [Biden 2024 reelection campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden_2024_presidential_campaign) on December 5 in [Boston](24437894.md). The demonstration was organized by the [Party for Socialism and Liberation](42034851.md).[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195]

### New York

On November 9, 2023, 200 Cornell students staged a [sit-in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sit-in) to symbolize the lives lost on both sides of the war.[^cite_note-196] On December 1, 2023, students at Cornell protested outside the office of university president [Martha Pollack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martha_Pollack), holding a [mock trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mock_trial) where they charged her with genocide against Palestinian civilians.[^cite_note-197] In March 2024, a group of 24 individuals, including two staff members, were arrested and charged with trespassing for staging a sit-in on the Cornell campus.[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199]

#### New York City

##### General protests

###### 2023



Pro-Palestine march in New York City, October 23, 2023



Stand with Palestine event, October 14, 2023

On October 8, 2023, one day after [Hamas'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7_October_attack) attack on Israel, hundreds of pro-Palestinian demonstrators—over 1,000 according to *[Politico](9172097.md)*[^cite_note-200]—participated in a rally in [Times Square](31389.md) organized by socialist and Palestine liberation groups, including the [Palestinian Youth Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Youth_Movement) and [Party for Socialism and Liberation](42034851.md).[^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202] Demonstrators waved Palestinian flags and repeated chants including "free, free Palestine, long live Palestine",[^cite_note-203] "Resistance is justified", "[Globalize the Intifada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalize_the_intifada)", "[From the river to the sea, Palestine will be free](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_the_river_to_the_sea)", "By any means necessary", "Glory to our martyrs", and "Smash the settler Zionist state".[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205][^cite_note-\:6-206] Dozens of pro-Israeli protesters also organized a counter-protest, singing [Hatikvah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hatikvah) and chanting "Never again" and "[Am Yisrael Chai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Am_Yisrael_Chai)".[^cite_note-\:6-206] Tensions were high between the 2 sides, with pro-Palestinian protesters chanting "700", referring to the confirmed number of Israeli fatalities in the attack at that moment, while the pro-Israeli protesters called them "terrorists" and "murderers".[^cite_note-\:6-206]

On October 14, thousands demonstrated against the bombing in Gaza.[^cite_note-207][^cite_note-208] A viral video from a pro-Israel protest showed several protesters explicitly calling for [genocide](12441.md) against Palestinians.[^cite_note-209] At [Dag Hammarskjöld Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dag_Hammarskj%C3%B6ld_Plaza), about 200 rallied on October 18 to draw attention to hostages held by Hamas. Protesters included many family members of hostages and local Jewish leaders.[^cite_note-hostagesUN-210] On November 6, around 500 protesters staged a [sit-in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sit-in) at the [Statue of Liberty](28617.md), calling for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-211]

Protesters gathered in front of the [New York Public Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Public_Library) on Thanksgiving Day. They clashed with police after being seen spray-painting [graffiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graffiti#Radical_and_political) on the building.[^cite_note-212] Pro-ceasefire protesters from Jewish Voice for Peace shut down the [Manhattan Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Bridge) for hours on November 26.[^cite_note-213] Protesters demonstrated at [Rockefeller Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockefeller_Center) on December 25 to call for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-214] Also on Christmas Day, pro-Palestinian protesters clashed with the NYPD and several were arrested.[^cite_note-215] On December 28, a vigil for children killed in Gaza was held in Times Square.[^cite_note-216] A "Flood the Bronx for Gaza" march was held in the Bronx.[^cite_note-217] Protesters blocked several bridges entering Manhattan on January 8, as well as a tunnel-entrance for New Jersey-bound traffic.[^cite_note-218][^cite_note-219]

###### 2024

On October 14, 2024, about 500 protesters organized by [Jewish Voice for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Voice_for_Peace) staged a sit-in outside the [New York Stock Exchange](21560.md) as the market opened Monday morning.[^cite_note-220] Multiple arrests were made.[^cite_note-221][^cite_note-222]

###### 2025

Main article: [2025 pro-Israel mob attack in Brooklyn § Protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_pro-Israel_mob_attack_in_Brooklyn#Protests)

On April 24, 2025, [clashes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_pro-Israel_mob_attack_in_Brooklyn) between pro-Palestinian and pro-Israeli demonstrators erupted outside the [Chabad-Lubavitch headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/770_Eastern_Parkway) in [Crown Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Heights), [Brooklyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn), in response to a visit by Israeli national security minister [Itamar Ben-Gvir](54504115.md).[^cite_note-\:13-223]

###### 2026



Pro-Palestine protesters in [Times Square](31389.md) in front of a state sponsored pro-Israel ad on the day of [Netanyahu's speech at the UN General Assembly](83334052.md), September 24, 2026

Protesters march through [Manhattan](45470.md) on the day of Netanyahu's speech, September 24, 2026

In May 2026, pro-Palestinian protesters gathered outside Park East Synagogue in Manhattan during the “[Great Israeli Real Estate Event](83112061.md)”, a property exhibition marketing homes in Israel and Israeli settlements in the West Bank. They waved Palestinian flags and chanted “There is only one solution, intifada revolution”.[^cite_note-224][^cite_note-225][^cite_note-226] They also chanted “Death to the IDF” and “It is right to resist, Israel should not exist”. The demonstration was described by organizers as opposing the sale of property in Israeli settlements, while some Jewish organizations and commentators criticized some of the slogans.[^cite_note-227][^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229]

##### Elected officials

A group of [Jewish Voice for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Voice_for_Peace) protesters, including children of [Holocaust survivors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_survivors), were arrested outside Senator [Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer)'s home for protesting against the bombings in Gaza.[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231] At [Brooklyn College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn_College), [Republican](32070.md) [New York City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_Council) member [Inna Vernikov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inna_Vernikov) of the [48th City Council District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City's_48th_City_Council_district) was arrested for bringing a gun to a counterprotest at a pro-Palestine rally.[^cite_note-232]

On October 24, a protest organized by the Bronx Anti-War Coalition took place outside of Rep. [Ritchie Torres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ritchie_Torres)' office in [Fordham, Bronx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fordham,_Bronx). Nearby, more than 500 pro-Israel protesters, including a large number of students at [SAR Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SAR_Academy), organized at nearby Seton Park to support the Congressman's position.[^cite_note-233]

On December 15, protesters gathered outside Chuck Schumer's office, chanting, "Schumer! Schumer! You can't hide. You're supporting genocide."[^cite_note-234] On February 7, 2024, Jewish protesters attempted to block Biden's presidential motorcade from attending a fundraiser with donors.[^cite_note-235] On February 15, protesters interrupted [Tom Suozzi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Suozzi)'s victory speech, chanting, "You can't hide! You're supporting genocide!"[^cite_note-236]

##### University protests

See also: [2024 Columbia University pro-Palestinian campus occupations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Columbia_University_pro-Palestinian_campus_occupations)



Vigil for Israel at Columbia University, October 9, 2023

On October 26, a group of anti-war students protested at [Cooper Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooper_Union).[^cite_note-Cooper_Union_students_were_not_barricaded-237] A group of Jewish students claimed they'd barricaded themselves in the library to hide from the "aggressive" protesters, but the [New York City Police Department](255413.md) said that there had been "no danger" to the Jewish students.[^cite_note-students_barricade_in_NYC-238][^cite_note-Cooper_Union_students_were_not_barricaded-237] The account told by the Jewish students was repeated by elected officials and media accounts, including condemnations from the [Anti-Defamation League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League), the [American Jewish Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Jewish_Committee), New York Governor [Kathy Hochul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathy_Hochul), and [Manhattan](45470.md) Borough President [Mark Levine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Levine_\(New_York_politician\)).[^cite_note-239][^cite_note-students_barricade_in_NYC-238][^cite_note-240]

Anti-war student protesters at [Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University) reported experiencing targeted harassment.[^cite_note-241] Students walked out of a class taught by [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton) in support of Palestine after her comments against a ceasefire.[^cite_note-242] Pro-Palestine protesters heckled Clinton on November 29 at Columbia University's International Affairs Building and accused her of supporting [genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_genocide_accusation).[^cite_note-243]

In January 2024, students at a pro-Palestine demonstration at Columbia University were attacked with [Skunk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skunk_\(weapon\)). One victim, who said that Palestinian friends recognized the odor as Skunk, described it as having the smell of "poop mixed with decaying animal".[^cite_note-244] Victims of the attack reported difficulty removing the odor from their clothes and other possessions, and that the effects of the spray including its odor, skin irritation, nausea, and dizziness continued days after the attack despite efforts to remove it.[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246] In response, demonstrators organized a protest outside the university.[^cite_note-247] The incident was being investigated by the New York City Police Department as a potential hate crime.[^cite_note-248] [Students for Justice in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Students_for_Justice_in_Palestine) and [Jewish Voice for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Voice_for_Peace) published a report in which they stated the perpetrators were former IDF soldiers and current Columbia students.[^cite_note-249]

At a pro-Palestinian rally held by students of Columbia University, protesters chanted "there is only one solution, [intifada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intifada) revolution", "Yemen, Yemen make us proud, turn another ship around" (a pro-[Houthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthi_movement) slogan) and "NYPD, [KKK](16779.md), [IDF](39237.md), they're all the same".[^cite_note-250]



NYPD cleaning the "Gaza Solidarity Encampment", April 18, 2024

On April 17, the [campus occupation by student protestors at Columbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_at_Columbia_University) began, with protestors establishing the "Gaza Solidarity Encampment", which was dismantled by the NYPD but later set up again on another part of campus.[^cite_note-251]

Protests later spread to several other campuses, including Yale and NYU.[^cite_note-252][^cite_note-253]

On April 26, a Columbia student who had emerged as a leader of the protest movement was barred after declaring in a video that "Zionists don't deserve to live". Other protests groups condemned the comments, but the *New York Times* said they raised the question of "How much of the movement in support of the Palestinian people in Gaza is tainted by antisemitism?"[^cite_note-254] The Columbia encampment and many other encampments at American university campuses were ultimately cleared by police.

##### Institution protests

On November 10, protesters staged a sit-in at *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, saying the newspaper was complicit in "laundering genocide".[^cite_note-255] The CEO of [the Legal Aid Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Legal_Aid_Society) said a resolution by New York City public defenders to condemn the killing of Palestinian civilians was antisemitic.[^cite_note-256] On December 7, [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md) held a vigil outside [United Nations headquarters](188955.md) in honor of the medical workers killed in Gaza.[^cite_note-257] Thousands of members of US labor unions gathered on December 22 in support of a ceasefire.[^cite_note-258] Rabbis for Ceasefire prayed for a ceasefire at the UN on January 9.[^cite_note-259] Protesters demonstrated outside of [Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_Sloan_Kettering_Cancer_Center) for its collaborations with Israeli medical centers.[^cite_note-260] On February 11, protesters at the [Museum of Modern Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Modern_Art) and [Brooklyn Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn_Museum) called on the institutions to cut ties with donors who had ties to Israel.[^cite_note-261] Protesters demonstrated against [AIPAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIPAC) at the lobby's New York office on February 23.[^cite_note-262] On March 14, protesters blocked *The New York Times'* distribution center.[^cite_note-263] In April, ads on [MTA](384273.md) subways were replaced with pro-Palestinian messages.[^cite_note-264]

##### Event protests

On November 23, 2023, pro-Palestinian protesters disrupted the [Thanksgiving Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macy's_Thanksgiving_Day_Parade). Wearing white jumpsuits covered with fake blood, some glued themselves to the parade route along [Sixth Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Avenue) near 45th street. Protesters were reported at two other locations.[^cite_note-265] Pro-Palestine demonstrators held a vigil and a rally outside the [Rockefeller Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockefeller_Center) during the annual lighting of the [Rockefeller Center Christmas tree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockefeller_Center_Christmas_Tree).[^cite_note-\:2-266] Police clashed with protesters at Sixth Street and seven people were arrested.[^cite_note-267] On March 28, pro-Palestinian protests occurred outside a Biden fundraiser at [Radio City Music Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_City_Music_Hall).[^cite_note-268]

### New Jersey

On October 22, 2023, an "emergency rally for Gaza" was held in [Paterson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paterson,_New_Jersey).[^cite_note-269] On October 28, a [Princeton University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princeton_University) staff member assaulted a pro-Palestinian protester at an off-campus event calling for ceasefire in Gaza.[^cite_note-270]

On February 12, protesters organized by "American Muslims for Palestine" interrupted an event inside [Drumthwacket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drumthwacket), the Governor's Mansion in Trenton where Gov. [Phil Murphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Murphy) was hosting a celebration of the lunar new year. The group demanded that the New Jersey-Israeli State Commission be disbanded.[^cite_note-271]

On October 17, 2025, protestors with Jewish Voice for Peace occupied the lobby of a high rise where an office of [Cory Booker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cory_Booker) is located, in protest of his votes in favor of military aid for Israel and against funding for [UNRWA](32270.md).[^cite_note-272]

### Pennsylvania

On October 9, 2023, a pro-Israel demonstration was held outside the Kaiserman Jewish Community Center in [Wynnewood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wynnewood,_Pennsylvania).[^cite_note-\:4-273]

On February 24, pro-Israel and pro-Palestine demonstrations were held simultaneously in [Lower Merion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_Merion_Township,_Pennsylvania). The two groups largely avoided one another, though they briefly exchanged chants.[^cite_note-274]

In late April 2024, pro-Palestinian students set up protest encampments on the grounds of [Swarthmore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swarthmore_College) and [Haverford College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haverford_College).[^cite_note-275]

#### Philadelphia

In the immediate aftermath of the [October 7 attacks](74998317.md), most of Philadelphia's major Jewish institutions made statements in support of Israel, including the [Jewish Federation of Greater Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Federation_of_Greater_Philadelphia?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Congregation Rodeph Shalom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congregation_Rodeph_Shalom_\(Philadelphia\)), and the [Weitzman National Museum of American Jewish History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weitzman_National_Museum_of_American_Jewish_History).

Most pro-Palestinian demonstrations in Philadelphia were organized or supported by the [Philly Palestine Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philly_Palestine_Coalition?action=edit\&redlink=1). The Coalition held its first pro-Palestinian rally since the start of the war on October 8.[^cite_note-\:4-273]

On October 12, pro-Palestinian demonstrators rallied outside [WHYY](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WHYY-FM)'s Old City studio in protest of local media's coverage of the Israeli-Palestinian Conflict.[^cite_note-276]

Large pro-Palestinian demonstrations were held on the [Benjamin Franklin Parkway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Franklin_Parkway) and at the [Philadelphia Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Museum_of_Art) on October 21 and in [Center City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_City,_Philadelphia) on October 28.[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278]

Multiple pro-Palestinian protests in late October were held outside the office of Senator [John Fetterman](5303368.md).[^cite_note-279]

On November 2, around 25 pro-Palestinian protestors were arrested after blocking the entrances to several Amtrak gates during a larger demonstration at [30th Street Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/30th_Street_Station).[^cite_note-280]

On November 9, pro-Palestinian protestors held a [die-in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Die-in) at [City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_City_Hall).[^cite_note-281] Another demonstration was held outside City Hall on November 17.[^cite_note-282] Another was held on November 24.[^cite_note-283]

On December 3, pro-Palestinian protestors held a citywide demonstration with several stops, including Goldie, an Israeli restaurant owned by [Michael Solomonov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Solomonov). Chants directed at the restaurant by protestors were condemned by politicians, including the [Biden administration](65732174.md), Governor [Josh Shapiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josh_Shapiro), Senator John Fetterman, Representative [Brendan Boyle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brendan_Boyle), State Representative [Ben Waxman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Waxman), and State Senator [Nikil Saval](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikil_Saval). In their statements, politicians said that the restaurant was targeted for being Jewish-owned. The Philly Palestine Coalition denied this accusation, citing Solomonov's financial support for the Friends of [United Hatzalah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Hatzalah), an emergency services organization partnered with the [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md), Solomonov's Israeli government-appointed role as a "culinary ambassador", and allegations by former Goldie employees that they were fired for expressing pro-Palestinian beliefs.[^cite_note-284]

On December 10, a pro-Israel rally was held at Congregation Rodeph Shalom.[^cite_note-285]

On December 14, pro-Palestinian protestors were arrested after blocking [I-76](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_76_\(Ohio%E2%80%93New_Jersey\)) and the [Spring Garden Street Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spring_Garden_Street_Bridge).[^cite_note-286]

On February 13, a large pro-Palestinian demonstration marched through Center City.[^cite_note-287]

On March 2, a pro-Palestinian protest, which began at City Hall, blocked the Benjamin Franklin Bridge for about 40 minutes. On March 3, a pro-Israel rally was held in [Rittenhouse Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rittenhouse_Square).[^cite_note-288]

On March 9, a pro-Palestinian demonstration marched from Rittenhouse Square to the Spring Garden Street offices of [Day & Zimmermann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Day_&_Zimmermann), a defense contractor manufacturing weapons used in the [Israeli invasion of Gaza](74998519.md).[^cite_note-289]

On March 30, a protest was held in Center City with hundreds in attendance. 67 people were arrested for disorderly conduct after blocking I-676.[^cite_note-290]

On April 15, pro-Palestinian protestors engaged in a series of [Tax Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_Day) actions intended to "block the flow of capital" and protest US taxpayer funding for Israel. Protestors slowed traffic and staged a car caravan funeral procession on I-95. They also blocked several highway ramps at Broad and Vine Streets and protested at City Hall, the IRS offices at 30th and Market Streets, and the Day & Zimmermann headquarters at 15th and Spring Garden Streets.[^cite_note-291]

On April 25, after a march from Center City to [West Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Philadelphia), which passed through the campuses of [Temple University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_University) and [Drexel University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drexel_University), pro-Palestinian protestors set up an encampment on the campus of the [University of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pennsylvania).[^cite_note-\:5-292] In the following days, the encampment grew in defiance of orders from the university administration to disband. Protestors vowed not to comply with ID checks by Penn police. A letter circulated by Penn Faculty for Justice in Palestine in support of the encampment collected more than 1,200 signatures.[^cite_note-\:5-292]

Six people were arrested during a pro-Palestinian [4th of July](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Day_\(United_States\)) demonstration at City Hall, after protestors burned flags and scuffled with police.[^cite_note-293]

Several hundred protestors gathered at City Hall on the night of the [2024 presidential debate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_presidential_debates) to protest the pro-Israel positions of both [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris). Marching down Market Street, the protest made it to 4th Street before being hemmed in by riot police. Organizers told marchers to disperse, but a smaller number stayed behind and clashed with police.[^cite_note-294]

### Rhode Island

On November 9, 20 Jewish students were arrested at [Brown University](4157.md) for occupying [University Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_Hall,_Brown_University) and demanding a ceasefire and [divestment resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disinvestment_from_Israel).[^cite_note-295] Charges against the Jewish students were dropped after a [Palestinian-American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian-American) Brown University student was [shot and wounded in Burlington, Vermont](75420817.md).[^cite_note-296] At a vigil for the injured student, protesters chanted “Shame! Shame! Shame!” at president [Christina Paxson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christina_Paxson), leading her to leave the podium.[^cite_note-297] On December 6, 41 protesters at Brown were charged with trespassing at University Hall for another sit-in protest to raise awareness about the same shooting in Burlington over Thanksgiving Break. On December 19, the university announced they would not be dropping the charges against the 41.[^cite_note-298] In February 2024, students at Brown announced they would be going on a [hunger strike](222292.md) to protest their university's complicity in the war.[^cite_note-299]

### Vermont

In [Burlington, Vermont](67258.md), large rallies were held in support of Israel and Palestine in October 2023. The Vermont chapter of the Party for Socialism and Liberation and Vermonters for Justice in Palestine held a pro-Palestinian rally. Governor [Phil Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Scott), mayor [Miro Weinberger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miro_Weinberger) and city council president Karen Paul attended a pro-Israel rally on Church Street organized by a coalition of local Jewish leaders.[^cite_note-300]

On November 9, protestors disrupted a fundraising event for [Becca Balint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Becca_Balint), demanding that she call for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-301]

In April 2024, students at the [University of Vermont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Vermont) and [Middlebury College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middlebury_College) established protest encampments, urging their universities to divest from Israel.[^cite_note-302]

## South

### Florida

At the [University of Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Florida), Jewish students held a candlelight vigil.[^cite_note-303] At the [University of South Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_South_Florida) in Tampa Bay, at least 200 marched at an Emergency Rally for Gaza following an official letter from the Chancellor of the [State University System of Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_University_System_of_Florida) directing all SUS Presidents to terminate all pro-Palestinian groups.[^cite_note-\:0-304][^cite_note-305][^cite_note-\:1-306]

### Georgia

In Atlanta, protesters gathered along the presidential [motorcade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorcade) route to [Rosalynn Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosalynn_Carter)'s funeral on November 28, calling on Biden to end all aid to Israel.[^cite_note-307][^cite_note-308] A woman at the Israeli consulate in Atlanta [self-immolated](916669.md) in protest of the war and was described to be in a "critical condition" on December 1.[^cite_note-309]

In late April 2024, at a protest at [Emory University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emory_University) in [Atlanta](3138.md), philosophy professor Noëlle McAfee and economics professor [Caroline Fohlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caroline_Fohlin) were arrested by police, according to CNN; after Fohlin questioned the police's use of force against people she identified as students, she was taken down to the ground by police and handcuffed.[^cite_note-310]

In June, 28 Pro-Palestinian protesters rallied in Atlanta where the first 2024 presidential debate was being held between Joe Biden and Donald Trump.[^cite_note-311]

### Kentucky

In Kentucky, the Students for Justice in Palestine organized a protest at the [University of Louisville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Louisville).[^cite_note-312]

### Louisiana

During [Kendrick Lamar](29909823.md)'s [halftime show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LIX_halftime_show) at [Super Bowl LIX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LIX), held in [Caesars Superdome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caesars_Superdome) in [New Orleans](53842.md), one backup dancer stood on top of a [Buick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buick) that was part of the performance and raised the [flags of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Palestine) and [Sudan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Sudan). The protester was tackled by security and banned from all NFL events, though he was not charged with a crime.[^cite_note-313]

### North Carolina

Clashes between supporters of Israel and Palestine were reported at the [University of North Carolina at Chapel Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_North_Carolina_at_Chapel_Hill), in front of the Wilson Library on October 12.[^cite_note-314] On Black Friday in [Raleigh, North Carolina](57845.md), people inside Crabtree Valley Mall made a false report to 911 that shots had been fired at a pro-Palestine protest. A large police response broke up the protest.[^cite_note-315]

### South Carolina

On January 8, 2024, Biden was heckled with chants of "ceasefire now" during a campaign speech at [Emanuel African Methodist Episcopal Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emanuel_African_Methodist_Episcopal_Church) in [South Carolina](27956.md). Biden responded, "And I've been quietly working, I've been quietly working with the Israeli government to get them to reduce and significantly get out of Gaza using all that I can to do that. I understand the passion."[^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318]

### Tennessee

On March 26, students at [Vanderbilt University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanderbilt_University) staged a sit-in at Chancellor Daniel Diermeier's office at Kirkland Hall after the school removed an amendment to prevent student government funds from funding Israel-supporting businesses. The amendment had garnered well over the required 600 student signatures. About 30 students partook in the protest and were forcibly removed the next day, 3 were arrested, including a reporter,[^cite_note-319] and multiple faced suspensions and imminent evictions from student housing.[^cite_note-320]

### Texas



Rally in support of Palestine at the [Texas State Capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_State_Capitol) in [Austin](1998.md), November 12, 2023

On November 12, thousands protested Texas's support of Israel in front of the [State Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_State_Capitol) in Austin.[^cite_note-321]



Protest against the [SXSW festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_by_Southwest) in Austin, Texas, March 14, 2024

More than 80 musicians decided to boycott the 2024 [SXSW music festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_by_Southwest) because of the event's partnership with the US military and major defense contractors.[^cite_note-322][^cite_note-323] The festival terminated its partnership with the [US Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Army) and the defense contractor [RTX Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTX_Corporation) for its 2025 festival.[^cite_note-324]
