On April 5, protesters gathered around [Harris County Jail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harris_County_Jail) in Houston to demand the release of protesters arrested during a separate [Al Quds Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quds_Day) demonstration.[^cite_note-325]

## West



Pro-Israel protest in [Salt Lake City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salt_Lake_City), Utah, October 11, 2023

### Alaska

On February 24, about 150 protesters calling for a ceasefire in Gaza, including indigenous elders and [Juneau Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juneau_Assembly) members Christine Woll and Paul Kelly, rallied at Marine Park in [Juneau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juneau,_Alaska).[^cite_note-326]

On May 6, the [Anchorage Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anchorage_Assembly) passed a resolution (8–4) calling for "a lasting peace in the Israeli-Hamas conflict".[^cite_note-327]

On May 18, about 100 protesters carrying signs calling for a ceasefire gathered for a rally outside of the [Alaska State Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_State_Capitol) in Juneau.[^cite_note-328] Governor [Mike Dunleavy](40933016.md) had recently proposed a bill that would criminalize unpermitted street protests.[^cite_note-329]

### Arizona

At the [University of Arizona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Arizona), a rally protesting against the bombing of Gaza was cancelled due to safety fears after university president [Robert C. Robbins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_C._Robbins) criticized its organizing group and urged them to conduct their protest "peacefully, safely, and civilly".[^cite_note-330] In March 2024, a young woman was dragged out of an event by security after interrupting [Jill Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jill_Biden) by stating, "Jill, when are you and the president going to call for a ceasefire in Gaza?"[^cite_note-331]

### California



Protest outside [Raytheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTX_Corporation)'s office in [Goleta, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goleta,_California), November 9, 2023

On November 18, 2023, the [California Democratic Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Democratic_Party) in Sacramento was cancelled after hundreds of pro-Palestinian protesters descended on the [SAFE Credit Union Convention Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacramento_Convention_Center_Complex).[^cite_note-332] On December 5, a Holiday tree-lighting ceremony at the [California State Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Capitol) was postponed due to a planned protest organized by the Sacramento Regional Coalition for Palestinian Rights.[^cite_note-333] On January 3, 2024, hundreds of protesters from Jewish anti-war groups, including [Sacramento City Councilwoman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacramento_City_Council) Katie Valenzuela, shut down the [California State Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Assembly) at the Capitol in Sacramento on the House's first working session of the new year.[^cite_note-334] In June 2024, protesters hung a banner reading "Stop the Genocide" on [El Capitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Capitan) in [Yosemite National Park](48664.md).[^cite_note-335]

#### Greater Los Angeles



Pro-Palestinian protest in Los Angeles against the war in Gaza and Hollywood's role in [dehumanizing Muslims](162173.md), November 2023

On October 9, thousands of demonstrators expressed solidarity with Israel on [Santa Monica Boulevard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Monica_Boulevard) in [Beverly Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beverly_Hills,_California).[^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337] A [UCLA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_Los_Angeles) student demonstrating support for Palestine was reportedly threatened with a knife.[^cite_note-338] On October 14, thousands protested against the bombings in Gaza in [Downtown Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Los_Angeles).[^cite_note-339] On October 21, thousands of demonstrators protested Israeli airstrikes, denouncing Netanyahu as a "war criminal".[^cite_note-340] On October 29, a pro-Israeli rally was held on [Santa Monica Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Monica_State_Beach).[^cite_note-341] On November 4, hundreds of demonstrators marched to the Israeli Consulate in [West Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Los_Angeles), calling for an immediate ceasefire.[^cite_note-342] In [Thousand Oaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thousand_Oaks,_California), a [69-year-old Jewish man named Paul Kessler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Paul_Kessler) died after sustaining head injuries following an altercation during dueling protests on November 4.[^cite_note-343] On November 16, suspect Loay Alnaji was arrested on charges of involuntary manslaughter and battery with a preliminary hearing set for December 4.[^cite_note-344]

Protesters splashed red paint on the [Raytheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raytheon) headquarters entrance sign in [El Segundo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Segundo,_California).[^cite_note-345][^cite_note-346] On Black Friday, hundreds gathered at [Pan Pacific Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan_Pacific_Park) in the [Fairfax District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairfax_District,_Los_Angeles) to attend a protest organized by [A.N.S.W.E.R.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A.N.S.W.E.R.) to demand a ceasefire in Gaza.[^cite_note-347] On December 7, the UCLA chapter of Jewish Voice for Peace held a "Chanukah for Peace" event.[^cite_note-348]



Protests against the [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive) in [Los Angeles](18110.md) at the [Wilshire Federal Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilshire_Federal_Building) in February 2024

Over a thousand pro-Palestine protesters assembled outside Biden's December 8 campaign fundraiser event.[^cite_note-349] [LAPD](218985.md) used the term "first amendment events"[^cite_note-350] in regard to the protest, but declared the demonstration "unlawful" at 5\:45 PM.[^cite_note-351] On December 14, 75 Jewish protesters were arrested for blocking the [110 Freeway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_110_and_State_Route_110_\(California\)).[^cite_note-352]

On January 1, 2024, about 50 protesters calling for a ceasefire and "justice for Palestine" stopped the [Rose Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rose_Parade) in Pasadena for 10 minutes.[^cite_note-353][^cite_note-354] [Khalid Abdalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalid_Abdalla) wore a white armband and a dove pin to the [81st Golden Globe Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/81st_Golden_Globes) to call for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-355] Hundreds protested outside the [Grammy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/66th_Annual_Grammy_Awards) calling a ceasefire, while singer [Annie Lennox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annie_Lennox) called for a ceasefire on stage.[^cite_note-356]

On February 12, hundreds protested the treatment of Palestinians in Gaza in a "Hands off Rafah" protest outside the [Wilshire Federal Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilshire_Federal_Building) in [Westwood](374055.md).[^cite_note-357]



A bloodied protester at a demonstration, in front of Adas Torah synagogue in Los Angeles, which was hosting an event about real estate in Israel and [occupied West Bank](59076253.md). There were violent clashes between the anti-Israel participants and pro-Israel counter-protesters, June 23, 2024

On March 10, about a thousand people blocked streets at the [Dolby Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolby_Theatre), delaying attendees' arrival at the [96th Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/96th_Academy_Awards). The largest groups organizing the protest belonged to "Film Workers for Palestine" and "SAG-AFTRA Members for Ceasefire".[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359] Celebrities at the event, including Oscar nominees and winners, wore Palestinian flag pins and red buttons distributed by an advocacy group called [Artists4Ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artists4Ceasefire).[^cite_note-360]

On April 10, a student led group called *Pomona Divest from Apartheid* at [Pomona College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pomona_College) occupied an administration building.[^cite_note-361]

On May 27, pro-Palestinian protesters blocked the [101 Freeway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/101_Freeway) in downtown Los Angeles.[^cite_note-362]

On June 23, pro-Palestinian demonstrators attempted to block people from entering the main entrance of the Adas Torah synagogue, which was hosting a seminar about real estate in Israel and the West Bank.[^cite_note-363] The demonstration deteriorated into violent clashes between the anti-Israel participants and pro-Israel counter-protesters. President Biden tweeted "I'm appalled by the scenes outside of Adas Torah synagogue in Los Angeles. Intimidating Jewish congregants is dangerous, unconscionable, antisemitic, and un-American."[^cite_note-364][^cite_note-365]

On October 5, hundreds protested in [Pershing Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pershing_Square_\(Los_Angeles\)) as the anniversary of October 7 approached.[^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367]

#### San Diego

On October 15, hundreds demonstrated for Gaza outside the [San Diego County Administration Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Diego_County_Administration_Center).[^cite_note-368] On October 21, hundreds of protesters organized by San Diego Coalition for Palestine shut down streets downtown.[^cite_note-369] On November 9, hundreds protested outside the headquarters of defense contractor [Northrup Grumman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northrop_Grumman) demanding a ceasefire. Later that same day, medical students, staff and other workers at [UC San Diego School of Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UC_San_Diego_School_of_Medicine) staged a solidarity [walkout](2016860.md) to protest the [public health crisis in Gaza](75277791.md).[^cite_note-370] Both of these protests were part of the international "Shut it Down for Palestine" event.[^cite_note-371] On Black Friday, marchers participated in a [die-in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Die-in) at a mall in [Escondido](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Escondido,_California).[^cite_note-372] On December 2, around 125 pro-Palestinian protesters disrupted a tree-lighting ceremony in [Balboa Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balboa_Park_\(San_Diego\)).[^cite_note-373] On December 23, protesters calling for a ceasefire marched through Balboa Park for two and half hours.[^cite_note-374] On April 24, 2024, dozens of Jewish and Palestinian American protesters rallied in Balboa Park and then delivered letters calling for a ceasefire to [representative](19468510.md) [Sara Jacobs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sara_Jacobs)' home nearby.[^cite_note-375] On October 4, about one thousand protesters marched in a pro-Palestinian rally in downtown.[^cite_note-376]

#### San Francisco Bay Area

See also: [San Francisco–Oakland Bay Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco%E2%80%93Oakland_Bay_Bridge)

##### Public figures and officials

On October 16, District Attorney [Brooke Jenkins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooke_Jenkins) posted and then deleted a post on [X](9988187.md) where she called the demonstration of anti-war protesters a "pro-Hamas rally".[^cite_note-377] During the [APEC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia-Pacific_Economic_Cooperation) conference, protesters demonstrated outside a fundraiser attended by [Joe Biden](145422.md) and [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris), demanding an immediate ceasefire.[^cite_note-378] Dozens of [Code Pink](2440600.md) anti-war protesters gathered outside of [Nancy Pelosi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Pelosi)'s home on December 3, calling for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-379] 700 staffers at mostly progressive Jewish organizations published an open-letter to Biden, calling for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-380]

On January 29, 2024, Nancy Pelosi was filmed telling a protester to "Go back to China" after they asked her to a call for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-381] On February 23, protesters marched inside the [Fairmont Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairmont_San_Francisco), where Joe Biden was staying during a fundraising trip, chanting, "Biden, Biden you can’t hide, we charge you with genocide".[^cite_note-382] Protesters also demonstrated outside his fundraising event.[^cite_note-383] On May 20, about 30 protesters demonstrated outside a [Harvard Club of San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_Club) event that Nancy Pelosi was speaking at. One was arrested for disrupting the speech inside.[^cite_note-384]

##### Schools and universities

See also: [List of pro-Palestinian protests on university campuses in California in 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pro-Palestinian_protests_on_university_campuses_in_California_in_2024)



Pro-Israel rally at [Stanford University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanford_University), October 10, 2023

On October 18, high school students across San Francisco [walked out](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walk-out) of class in protest of the bombing of Gaza and to demand an immediate [ceasefire](592591.md).[^cite_note-385] [Fremont High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fremont_High_School_\(Oakland,_California\)) in Oakland drew headlines for raising the [Palestinian flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Palestine) on the campus' main flagpole.[^cite_note-386] Students at [UC Berkeley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_Berkeley) hung a banner with the Palestinian flag on [Sather Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sather_Tower) in an unauthorized protest.[^cite_note-387] In the [Central Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Valley_\(California\)), students at [Stanislaus State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanislaus_State), [UC Merced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UC_Merced) and [Fresno State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_University,_Fresno) held protests in late October.[^cite_note-388] On April 22, students at [Cal Poly Humboldt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cal_Poly_Humboldt) occupied an administration building "in solidarity with students across the nation...for Palestine". [Arcata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arcata,_California) City Councilmember Sara Schaefer called the massive police response "overkill".[^cite_note-389][^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391]

##### Rallies, protests, and marches

In San Francisco, several hundred peaceful protesters marched on October 15 in support of Gaza.[^cite_note-392] On October 19, the San Francisco chapter of [Jewish Voice for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Voice_for_Peace) chained themselves to the [San Francisco Federal Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Federal_Building) in protest at Israeli actions in Gaza.[^cite_note-393] On October 28, around 15,000 protesters marched in San Francisco, shutting down the [Central Freeway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Freeway) for more than an hour.[^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395] In advance of the [Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia-Pacific_Economic_Cooperation) summit, protesters marked a [self-driving car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-driving_car) with "Free Gaza".[^cite_note-396] On November 5, a Jewish-led group of activists protested a gala for the [Friends of the Israel Defense Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friends_of_the_Israel_Defense_Forces) at the [Hiller Aviation Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiller_Aviation_Museum) in [San Carlos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Carlos,_California).[^cite_note-397] Hundreds of anti-war protesters were arrested for occupying the [Oakland Federal Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_V._Dellums_Federal_Building).[^cite_note-398] On December 16, around 500 [Google](1092923.md) employees protested the company's \$1.2 billion [Project Nimbus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_Nimbus) contract with the Israeli military and government.[^cite_note-399]

On April 5, 2024, a rally organized by a group called "Vigil4Gaza" protested outside of the [Palo Alto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palo_Alto,_California) City Hall.[^cite_note-400] On April 17, nine Google employees were arrested on charges of trespassing after staging a sit-in at the company's Sunnyvale campus in protest against Project Nimbus.[^cite_note-401] On June 3, seventy people were arrested for occupying the [Israeli consulate in San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consulate_General_of_Israel_to_the_Pacific_Northwest_Region).[^cite_note-402]

##### Ports, roads, and bridges



Pro-Palestine protest in [Emeryville, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emeryville,_California), November 24, 2023

On November 3, 2023, 200 protesters at the [Port of Oakland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Oakland) chained themselves to the US military supply ship [MV *Cape Orlando*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MV_Cape_Orlando) to prevent its departure to Israel.[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404] Anti-war protesters chained themselves together, blocking all traffic on the [San Francisco–Oakland Bay Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco%E2%80%93Oakland_Bay_Bridge).[^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406] On December 6, hundreds of protesters laid children's shoes outside of the [Golden Gate Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Gate_Bridge) to symbolize children killed during the conflict. Afterwards, [interfaith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interfaith_dialogue) groups marched from both ends meeting in the middle to call for a ceasefire. One protester was arrested for climbing a flagpole and putting up a Palestine flag.[^cite_note-407][^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409] Protesters demonstrating against the planned [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive) blocked the Golden Gate Bridge on February 16, 2024.[^cite_note-410][^cite_note-411] On February 21, protesters blocked [Highway 101](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_101_in_California) to demand Biden to stop arming Israel and enact an immediate ceasefire.[^cite_note-412] In March 2024, a group of activists built a sign in [South San Francisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_San_Francisco,_California) visible from airplanes taking off from [San Francisco International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_International_Airport) (SFO) calling for an end of military aid to Israel.[^cite_note-413] Protesters at SFO shut down multiple security gates and roadways leading to the airport, holding banners that read "Stop Arming Israel".[^cite_note-414] On March 31, protesters chained themselves to the [USNS *Harvey Milk*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USNS_Harvey_Milk) at Pier 32 in San Francisco.[^cite_note-415]

### Colorado

In May 2025, a pro-Palestinian protest was held in [Denver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver) against Congressman [Jason Crow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason_Crow) for repeatedly accepting campaign donations from executives of [Palantir Technologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palantir), which provides intelligence and surveillance services to the [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) (IDF) in the Gaza war.[^cite_note-416]

### Idaho

In [Boise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boise,_Idaho), protesters set up an encampment in front of the [Idaho State Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idaho_State_Capitol) in early May 2024. The encampment was removed by police several days later.[^cite_note-417] On July 11, the director of the Idaho Department of Administration sued to repeal a 2014 [injunction](39343.md) allowing "symbolic tents" at the Idaho Capitol Annex. Protesters organized by Boise to Palestine, Friends of Idaho, BSU Students for Palestine and the People’s University were named in the suit.[^cite_note-418]

### Montana

In [Missoula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missoula), pro-Palestine protesters denounced [neo-Nazis](54361.md) who had tried to join their demonstration at the [Missoula County Courthouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missoula_County_Courthouse) and had called for Jews to be "stopped". Analysts said these actions were part of attempts of neo-Nazis and other far right groups to hijack pro-Palestine and anti-Israel events to promote anti-semitic and anti-immigrant ideals.[^cite_note-419]

### Oregon

On October 29, activists in [Portland](23503.md) protested against the war, asking US Senator [Jeff Merkley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Merkley) to call for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-420] Pro-Palestine students protested at [Portland State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portland_State_University) on November 8, calling for the university to cut ties with [Boeing](18933266.md), which sells weapons to Israel.[^cite_note-421] On November 17, Portland's chapter of the [Party for Socialism and Liberation](42034851.md) organized a protest that blocked the entrance to a Boeing manufacturing plant.[^cite_note-422]

On May 2, a group calling itself "Rachel Corrie's Ghost Brigade" reported that it had cut through a fence at a Portland police facility and burned 17 police cars. [Rachel Corrie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rachel_Corrie) was an American activist who died in 2003 while protesting the IDF's destruction of Palestinian houses in Gaza; she was crushed by an Israeli bulldozer and died.[^cite_note-423]

### Washington

On October 29, hundreds demonstrated in [Seattle](11388236.md)'s [Pioneer Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pioneer_Square,_Seattle) to support Israel's war on Hamas.[^cite_note-424] On November 3, [Jewish Voice for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Voice_for_Peace) activists in Seattle demonstrated outside the [Henry M. Jackson Federal Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_M._Jackson_Federal_Building), demanding US Senator [Patty Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patty_Murray) to call for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-425] On November 6, an estimated 300 people blocked the entrance of the [Port of Tacoma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Tacoma) to prevent longshoremen from loading suspected military equipment and weapons onto the MV *Cape Orlando*.[^cite_note-426][^cite_note-427] On November 11, a protest organized by the [Samidoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samidoun) blocked streets near [Westlake Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westlake_Center) in Seattle.[^cite_note-428] On November 19, hundreds called for a ceasefire at the [Space Needle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Needle). The rally was organized by Jewish Voice for Peace.[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430]

On December 7, [University of Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Washington) police in Seattle detained 36 people for occupying an administration building with demands that the school stop funding Israel and Boeing.[^cite_note-431][^cite_note-432] Protesters blocked and closed a highway on January 7, 2024.[^cite_note-433] On March 13, protesters demonstrated outside the Aerospace and Defense Summit to oppose Boeing's military ties with Israel.[^cite_note-434]

## Territories

On January 27, 2024, protesters gathered outside the offices of Delegate [James Moylan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Moylan) in [Hagåtña, Guam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hag%C3%A5t%C3%B1a,_Guam), calling for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-435]

In Puerto Rico, several dozen protesters gathered to protest against Vice President [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) during her visit to [San Juan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Juan,_Puerto_Rico) on March 22, 2024.[^cite_note-436]

In the United States Virgin Islands, a protest march was held on [Saint Croix](229431.md) on November 4, 2023.[^cite_note-437]

## Responses

### Universities

See also: [Gaza war protests at universities § Protests on campuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_at_universities#Protests_on_campuses), and [Reactions to the 2024 Gaza war protests at universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reactions_to_the_2024_Gaza_war_protests_at_universities)



Protester and counter-protester on [Sproul Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sproul_Plaza) at [UC Berkeley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UC_Berkeley), October 25, 2023

On November 6, [Brandeis University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandeis_University) became the first private university in the United States to ban its student chapter of [Students for Justice in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Students_for_Justice_in_Palestine), saying that the leading factor in their decision was "the SJP’s support of militant group Hamas". A student spokesperson for the SJP chapter disputed this claim, calling it "unsubstantiated" and saying "we have not received any evidence that can lead to the suppression of our right to free speech." [The Foundation for Individual Rights and Expression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foundation_for_Individual_Rights_and_Expression) said that "None of the chants or slogans cited by [President Liebowitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_D._Liebowitz) come close to meeting the legal criteria for incitement or harassment," and that "Brandeis is punishing its students for nothing more than protected political advocacy."[^cite_note-438] On November 10, [Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University) suspended its campus chapters of Students for Justice in Palestine and [Jewish Voice for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Voice_for_Peace) for repeated violations of campus event policies, thus barring them from hosting events on campus.[^cite_note-439] Columbia's decision was criticized by [Naomi Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naomi_Klein) and [Noura Erakat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noura_Erakat).[^cite_note-440] After [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University) president [Claudine Gay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claudine_Gay) condemned the slogan "[from the river to the sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_the_river_to_the_sea)", more than 100 faculty signed an open letter criticizing her censure.[^cite_note-441][^cite_note-442] On December 14, [Rutgers University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutgers_University) became the first public university to suspend its campus chapter of Students for Justice in Palestine in response to complaints of disruptive behavior and vandalism.[^cite_note-443] Donors to top universities in the United States withdrew millions of dollars in [donations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donation) in light of the universities' responses to Hamas' offensive.[^cite_note-444]

On October 24, [Ray Rodrigues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_Rodrigues), the Chancellor of the [State University System of Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_University_System_of_Florida) issued an order[^cite_note-\:0-304] to disband all existing chapters of National Students for Justice in Palestine (NSJP) in the system.[^cite_note-445][^cite_note-\:1-306] Chapters at [University of Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Florida) and [University of South Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_South_Florida) have filed federal lawsuits against several parties including Rodrigues and Gov. [Ron Desantis](36729527.md) on constitutional grounds over the move.[^cite_note-446] On December 5, the [Louisiana Department of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_Department_of_Education) opened a [civil-rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil-rights) investigation against [Tulane University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulane_University) after a fight occurred at a pro-Palestine rally on October 26.[^cite_note-447] [American University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_University) announced it was banning protests inside university buildings.[^cite_note-448] The [Muslim Legal Fund of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_Legal_Fund_of_America) sued Harvard University for failing to protect Muslim and Arab students from harassment.[^cite_note-449] The [American Civil Liberties Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_Liberties_Union) published an open letter urging colleges not to initiate "baseless investigations" into pro-Palestinian student groups.[^cite_note-450]

The [Israeli Ministry of Foreign Affairs](18133296.md) announced on November 25 it was forming a task force to combat "those generating antisemitism on campuses" in the United States.[^cite_note-451] In February 2024, the ACLU signed a letter to US Secretary of Education [Miguel Cardona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_Cardona) calling on him to reject redefining antisemitism to include political criticism of the government of the state of Israel, saying it would lead to [First Amendment](31653.md) violations.[^cite_note-452]



Protest against [antisemitism on American campuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universities_and_antisemitism) at [George Washington University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington_University) in Washington, D.C., May 2024

After Bret Gustafson, a [Washu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_University_in_St._Louis) professor, posted online in support of a protest outside of the home of the president of [AIPAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIPAC), the university stated that Gustafson's views do not reflect the university's, and that they would not tolerate "discrimination, harassment or threatening behavior".[^cite_note-453] Jairo Fúnez-Flores, a professor at [Texas Tech University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Tech_University), was suspended for his social media posts related to the conflict.[^cite_note-454] [Michel DeGraff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_DeGraff), a professor of linguistics at the [Massachusetts Institute of Technology](18879.md) was issued formal reprimands and denied a pay raise after he proposed to teach a course on language used to describe the Israeli-Palestinian conflict.[^cite_note-455] [Maura Finkelstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maura_Finkelstein) at [Muhlenberg College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhlenberg_College) was fired for reposting a post by [Remi Kanazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remi_Kanazi) on Instagram.[^cite_note-456]

[Elizabeth Magill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Magill), the president of the [University of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pennsylvania), resigned on December 9 following congressional testimony in which she said that the question of whether calls for an "intifada" or uses of the phrase "from the river to the sea" violate the university's code of conduct is a "context-dependent decision".[^cite_note-457][^cite_note-458] In January 2024, [Indiana University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_University) fired Abdulkader Sinno, a tenured political science professor, in response to political pressure from the state's Republican legislators.[^cite_note-459] Students at [Northwestern University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_University) faced criminal charges for publishing a pro-Palestinian parody newspaper under a law originally used to combat the [Ku Klux Klan](16779.md).[^cite_note-460] The New York chapter of the ACLU told Columbia University that the university must reinstate its chapters of Jewish Voice for Peace and Students for Justice in Palestine or face a legal challenge for First Amendment violations.[^cite_note-461] On March 12, the [New York Civil Liberties Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Civil_Liberties_Union) and [Palestine Legal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Legal) sued Columbia University for banning the groups.[^cite_note-462]

Students in the United States have reported fears of losing future job opportunities for engaging in protests against military action in Gaza.[^cite_note-463][^cite_note-464] By April 2024, hundreds of students had been arrested, suspended, put on probation, and expelled, including 50 students who had been arrested at Barnard.[^cite_note-465] In June 2024, legislators in Pennsylvania introduced a bill that would block state funding for any college or university that divests from "commercial financial activity in Israel", unless it were for poor investment performance.[^cite_note-466] In October 2024, the [ACLU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_Liberties_Union), [Amnesty International](18947898.md), and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) wrote a joint statement warning that law enforcement on 20 U.S. college campuses had used potentially excessive force against pro-Palestinian protestors, in violation of international law.[^cite_note-467]

In October 2025, [Kent Syverud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kent_Syverud), the chancellor of [Syracuse University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syracuse_University), stated that the protests on campus seemed to have been encouraged by [Iran](14653.md). He said that, although there was an initially welcoming atmosphere for both pro-Israel and pro-Palestinian activities during the early months of October 2023, a noticeable shift occurred later. Syverud expressed his belief that Iran played a significant role in fostering the demonstrations, saying out that there was little to no involvement from the university's own students in the protests.[^cite_note-468]

### Doxxing

The [Israel on Campus Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_on_Campus_Coalition) reportedly engaged in covert espionage against pro-Palestinian student organizations.[^cite_note-469] [Accuracy in Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accuracy_in_Media) sent [doxxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doxing) trucks to [Yale](34273.md) and Columbia, displaying the names and faces of students under a banner labeling them "leading antisemites" on campus.[^cite_note-470][^cite_note-471] [Canary Mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canary_Mission) published the identities and images of Harvard students involved in the issue of an open letter, published on October 7, that held "the Israeli regime entirely responsible for all unfolding violence".[^cite_note-472][^cite_note-473]

### Firings

[Susan Sarandon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susan_Sarandon) was dropped in November 2023 by [United Talent Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Talent_Agency) in response to comments she made at a pro-Palestinian rally she attended that month; Sarandon later apologized for the phrasing of her remarks.[^cite_note-\:2-266][^cite_note-474] A [Google](1092923.md) employee was fired for "interfering with an official company-sponsored event" after he interrupted [Barak Regev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barak_Regev?action=edit\&redlink=1) by stating, "I refuse to build technology that empowers genocide."[^cite_note-475] In May 2024, a nurse at [NYU Langone Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NYU_Langone_Health) was fired for calling the war a genocide.[^cite_note-476] In October 2024, [Microsoft](19001.md) employees were fired after organizing a vigil for Palestinians killed in Gaza.[^cite_note-477]

### Political



Pro-Palestinian protest in Columbus, Ohio, December 22, 2023

[Nancy Pelosi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Pelosi) drew condemnation after suggesting that some anti-war protesters were being funded by [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) and should be investigated by the FBI.[^cite_note-478] [Abdullah Hammoud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdullah_Hammoud), the Democratic mayor of [Dearborn, Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dearborn,_Michigan) (a city with one of the largest [Arab American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_American) communities), wrote: "So, based on Nancy Pelosi’s remarks, 76% of Democrats / 49% of Republicans / 61% of Americans are potentially paid operatives of Russia who are pushing Putin’s message of calling for a ceasefire?"[^cite_note-479]

In October 2024, [the Heritage Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Heritage_Foundation) released a document titled "Project Esther: A National Strategy to Combat Antisemitism", which detailed a plan to go after what it termed the "Hamas Support Network", consisting of nonprofits and activist groups such as [American Muslims for Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Muslims_for_Palestine), [Jewish Voice for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Voice_for_Peace), and [Students for Justice in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Students_for_Justice_in_Palestine).[^cite_note-480]

#### Lobbying

The [American Israel Public Affairs Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIPAC) sent an email to its supporters asking them to email their senators in opposition to a measure introduced by US Senator [Bernie Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders) to investigate Israel's human rights practices.[^cite_note-481]

On January 10, 2024, *[The Jewish Forward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Forward)* reported that the [Anti-Defamation League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League) was including pro-Palestine rallies in its tallies of anti-Semitic incidents in the United States.[^cite_note-482][^cite_note-483]



Protest against the [detention of Mahmoud Khalil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detention_of_Mahmoud_Khalil) in New York City, March 10, 2025

Civil rights groups American-Arab Anti-Discrimination Committee and Palestine Legal described the response to criticism of Israel in the United States as "[McCarthyite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McCarthyism)".[^cite_note-TGM-484]

#### Legislation

In January 2024, Senators [Thom Tillis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thom_Tillis) and [Marsha Blackburn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marsha_Blackburn) introduced the Safe and Open Streets Act to make it a federal crime to block a public road or highway, as a way to criminalize pro-Palestinian protests.[^cite_note-485]

In September 2024, [Nassau County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nassau_County,_New_York), New York passed a face mask ban, resulting in the arrest of a 26-year-old protester.[^cite_note-486]

The "[Antisemitism Awareness Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_Awareness_Act)", spearheaded by the Republicans but also backed by many Democrats, passed the [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md) in a 320–91 vote on May 1, 2024, and proceeded to the Senate.[^cite_note-487][^cite_note-488] The bill was intended to add the [International Holocaust Remembrance Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Holocaust_Remembrance_Alliance)'s approved [working definition of antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IHRA_definition_of_antisemitism) to [title VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Rights_Act_of_1964#Title_VI_%E2%80%93_nondiscrimination_in_federally_assisted_programs), which prohibits "exclusion from participation in, denial of benefits of, and discrimination under federally assisted programs on ground of race, color, or national origin".[^cite_note-489] Democratic Representative [Sara Jacobs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sara_Jacobs), who is Jewish, said she opposed the bill because "it fail\[ed] to effectively address the very real rise of antisemitism, all while defunding colleges and universities across the country and punishing many, if not all, of the non-violent protestors speaking out against the Israeli military’s conduct."[^cite_note-490]

The proposed legislation would have broadened the legal definition of antisemitism to include anti-[Zionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionism), criticism of the policies of the state of Israel, and concerns about Palestinian human rights, by categorizing all of that as hate speech, and it has been criticized for conflating "Judaism with Zionism in assuming that all Jews are Zionists" and automatic citizens of Israel rather than the U.S., thereby severely undermining genuine safety for Jewish citizens. It faced strong opposition from several Democratic lawmakers, Jewish organizations, and free speech advocates, including more than 800 Jewish U.S. academics, who signed a letter calling on Biden not to sign the bill.



Pro-Israel protest in Washington, D.C. in October 2024

[Jeremy Ben-Ami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeremy_Ben-Ami), president of the centrist pro-Israel group [J Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J_Street), said that his organization opposed the bill because it was an "unserious" effort led by Republicans "to continually force votes that divide the Democratic caucus on an issue that shouldn't be turned into a political football".[^cite_note-491]

The ACLU, in a press release on April 26, said the bill was an attack on [First Amendment](31653.md) rights and argued that its "overbroad" definition of antisemitism "could result in colleges and universities suppressing a wide variety of speech critical of Israel or in support of Palestinian rights in an effort to avoid investigations by the Department \[of Education] and the potential loss of funding".[^cite_note-492] Organizations like the [Anti-Defamation League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League) and [Conference of Presidents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conference_of_Presidents_of_Major_American_Jewish_Organizations) have praised the bill, and it is based on definitions by the International Holocaust Remembrance Alliance that have been criticized by 100 Israeli and international civil society organizations that wrote to the [United Nations](31769.md) Secretary General [Antonio Guterres](471806.md) in 2023 urging the UN not to adopt the definitions.[^cite_note-497]

The bill was never voted on in the Senate, but was reintroduced in the [119th Congress](70645797.md) by [Tim Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Scott).[^cite_note-498]

&#x20;\


#### Investigations

On January 24, 2024, [Palestine Legal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Legal) stated it had received reports of activists who had written social media posts "criticizing Israel's genocide of Palestinians in Gaza" being visited by the [FBI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FBI).[^cite_note-499] In March 2024, faculty at the [University of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pennsylvania) sued the school to prevent it from sending documents related to pro-Palestinian protests on campus to Congress, stating they opposed the "new form of [McCarthyism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McCarthyism), in which accusations of anti-Semitism are substituted for insinuations of Communist leanings which were the tool of oppression in the 1950s".[^cite_note-500]

### Lawsuits

In May 2024, nine Israelis sued American pro-Palestinian advocacy organizations in federal court in Virginia for spreading "propaganda" in the United States; the plaintiffs were made up of six survivors of the October 7 attack, one sibling of a victim of the attack, and two people who were displaced from their homes in the aftermath of the attack.[^cite_note-501] A lawyer for [American Muslims for Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Muslims_for_Palestine) called it "absolutely a threat to free speech", and "a threat to free speech on any front."[^cite_note-502] The lawsuit was dismissed by the court in February 2026.[^cite_note-503]

### Analysis

&#x20;For legal scholar Shirin Sinnar, the repression of campus protests from university administrators in the 2023–24 academic year facilitated authoritarianism under the [Trump administration](78298108.md), arguing that the "failure to rebut politicized narratives that pro-Palestine protests were rampant with violence, antisemitism, and lawlessness has made it harder for academic institutions to resist the even greater weaponization of such caricatures now." Sinnar adds:&#x20;

> The denunciation of these protests as violating the norms of civil disobedience, based on simplistic understandings of theories of civil disobedience, has made it easier to cast student protestors as agents of chaos and disorder without a moral message and demand on society. It is far easier to punish higher education institutions for permitting protests—and to disappear students from their dorm rooms and the streets—when campus protestors have been vilified over the course of more than a year. The new speech and protest policies that many colleges and universities adopted, even before the Trump administration returned to power, challenged the rule of law within these institutions, whether viewed in formalist, substantive, governance, or procedural terms. Having ceded so much ground already, it is a small step for some colleges and universities to acquiesce entirely to the relentless threats now levied against them. Such acquiescence will not only undermine these institutions' missions of research and teaching, but also undercut their ability to act as an independent preserve of inquiry and dissent in the face of mounting authoritarianism.[^cite_note-504]&#x20;

[^cite_note-504]: &#x20;Sinnar, Shirin (August 14, 2025). ["Campus Protests and the Rule of Law"](https://scholarship.law.duke.edu/lcp/vol87/iss3/16). *Law and Contemporary Problems*. **87** (3): 117–157. [ISSN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISSN_\(identifier\)) [0023-9186](https://search.worldcat.org/issn/0023-9186). As federal threats to academic freedom continue to escalate during the Trump administration, the failure to uphold institutional values since the 2023- 24 academic year has dangerously exposed colleges and universities to these new pressures. The failure to rebut politicized narratives that pro-Palestine protests were rampant with violence, antisemitism, and lawlessness has made it harder for academic institutions to resist the even greater weaponization of such caricatures now. The denunciation of these protests as violating the norms of civil disobedience, based on simplistic understandings of theories of civil disobedience, has made it easier to cast student protestors as agents of chaos and disorder without a moral message and demand on society. It is far easier to punish higher education institutions for permitting protests—and to disappear students from their dorm rooms and the streets—when campus protestors have been vilified over the course of more than a year. The new speech and protest policies that many colleges and universities adopted, even before the Trump administration returned to power, challenged the rule of law within these institutions, whether viewed in formalist, substantive, governance, or procedural terms. Having ceded so much ground already, it is a small step for some colleges and universities to acquiesce entirely to the relentless threats now levied against them. Such acquiescence will not only undermine these institutions' missions of research and teaching, but also undercut their ability to act as an independent preserve of inquiry and dissent in the face of mounting authoritarianism.
